### Save Compose State with the saveable delegate in SavedStateHandle

Source: https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/viewmodel/viewmodel-savedstate

Use the saveable delegate from the lifecycle-viewmodel-compose artifact to interoperate between SavedStateHandle and Compose's Saver APIs. Any State saveable via rememberSaveable with a custom Saver can also be saved with SavedStateHandle.

```kotlin
class SavedStateViewModel(private val savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle) : ViewModel() {

    var filteredData: List<String> by savedStateHandle.saveable {
        mutableStateOf(emptyList())
    }

    fun setQuery(query: String) {
        withMutableSnapshot {
            filteredData += query
        }
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Save Compose State with SavedStateHandle saveable()

Source: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/state-saving

Use the experimental saveable API to store a TextFieldValue as MutableState in SavedStateHandle so it survives activity and process recreation. The stateSaver parameter accepts custom savers like rememberSaveable().

```kotlin
class ConversationViewModel(
    savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle
) : ViewModel() {

    var message by savedStateHandle.saveable(stateSaver = TextFieldValue.Saver) {
        mutableStateOf(TextFieldValue(""))
    }
        private set

    fun update(newMessage: TextFieldValue) {
        message = newMessage
    }

    /*...*/
}

val viewModel = ConversationViewModel(SavedStateHandle())

@Composable
fun UserInput(/*...*/) {
    TextField(
        value = viewModel.message,
        onValueChange = { viewModel.update(it) }
    )
}SavingUIStateSnippets.kt
```

--------------------------------

### Provide custom back navigation > Implement custom back navigation in Compose > Composable tree interception

Source: https://developer.android.com/guide/navigation/custom-back

If you have multiple enabled `BackHandler` composables at different levels of your composable tree, only the innermost one intercepts the back event.

9: /**
10:  * The analytics switch must be a control, not decoration.
11:  *
12:  * Firebase Analytics is bundled in both flavours but automatic provider startup is removed.
13:  * Collection requires a current explicit choice. This lock ties together the consent
14:  * owner that calls the SDK, the startup path that re-applies the stored choice, and the Settings
15:  * toggle that writes through it.
16:  *
17:  * Legal copy is checked in every shipped locale because the previous text ("No analytics SDK is
18:  * bundled") was translated five times — fixing only the English file would leave four languages
19:  * telling users the opposite of the truth.
20:  */
21: class AnalyticsConsentTest {
22: 
23:     private fun source(relative: String): String {
24:         val candidates = listOf(File(relative), File("app/$relative"))
25:         return candidates.firstOrNull { it.isFile }?.readText()
26:             ?: error("missing source under test: $relative")
27:     }
28: 
29:     private fun legalBody(locale: String): String {
30:         val file = listOf(
31:             File("src/main/res/$locale/strings_legal.xml"),
32:             File("app/src/main/res/$locale/strings_legal.xml")
33:         ).firstOrNull { it.isFile } ?: error("missing strings_legal.xml for $locale")
34:         val match = Regex("""<string name="legal_p_analytics_body">(.*?)</string>""", RegexOption.DOT_MATCHES_ALL)
35:             .find(file.readText())
36:         assertTrue("legal_p_analytics_body missing from $locale", match != null)
37:         return match!!.groupValues[1]
38:     }
39: 
40:     @Test
41:     fun theConsentOwnerDrivesTheBundledSdk() {
42:         val consent = source("src/main/java/com/catsmoker/app/application/privacy/AnalyticsConsent.kt")
43:         assertTrue(
44:             "the stored choice must reach Firebase",
45:             consent.contains("setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled")
46:         )
47:         assertTrue("the key the Settings screen already shipped stays the key", consent.contains("analytics_enabled"))
48:         assertEquals(
49:             "Settings and the SDK must agree on the default",
50:             true,
51:             consent.contains("DEFAULT_ENABLED = false")
52:         )
53:         assertTrue(consent.contains("PrivacyPolicy.analyticsAllowed"))
54:     }
55: 
56:     @Test
57:     fun startupReappliesTheStoredChoice() {
58:         val app = source("src/main/java/com/catsmoker/app/system/CatsmokerApp.kt")
59:         assertTrue(
60:             "a choice made in a previous process must be honoured before collection starts",
61:             app.contains("AnalyticsConsent.sync(this)")
62:         )
63:     }
64: 
65:     @Test
66:     fun settingsWritesThroughTheConsentOwnerInsteadOfAPreferencesOnlyFlag() {
67:         val vm = source("src/main/java/com/catsmoker/app/screens/settings/SettingsViewModel.kt")
68:         assertTrue(vm.contains("AnalyticsConsent.setEnabled(context, enabled)"))
69:         assertFalse(
70:             "the decorative preference write is the defect this change removed",
71:             vm.contains("putBoolean(PREF_ANALYTICS")
72:         )
73:         assertTrue(
74:             "the toggle's initial state comes from the same owner",
75:             vm.contains("AnalyticsConsent.isEnabled(context)")
76:         )
77:     }
78: 
79:     @Test
80:     fun everyLocaleDescribesTheBundledSdkTruthfully() {
81:         val locales = listOf("values", "values-en-rGB", "values-ar", "values-es", "values-ja", "values-zh-rCN", "values-pt-rBR")
82:         locales.forEach { locale ->
83:             val body = legalBody(locale)
84:             assertTrue("$locale must name the bundled SDK", body.contains("Firebase Analytics"))
85:             assertFalse(
86:                 "$locale still claims no analytics SDK is bundled",
87:                 body.contains("No analytics SDK", ignoreCase = true)
88:             )
89:             assertFalse(
90:                 "$locale still claims no usage data is transmitted",
91:                 body.contains("no usage data is transmitted", ignoreCase = true)
92:             )
93:         }
94:     }
95: }

8: /**
9:  * The three first-run screens are deliberately non-scrollable: every step must be readable at
10:  * a glance, including on the shortest supported phone. A scroll container is the easy way to
11:  * "fix" a layout that no longer fits, so this test fails the build if one is reintroduced.
12:  *
13:  * The two long steps (Terms, Permissions) do keep one deliberate exception: in landscape, or on
14:  * any viewport shorter than ~420 dp, the *inner* document/grid scrolls inside a
15:  * `maxHeight < 420.dp` branch so the action row and footer stay reachable. That branch is a
16:  * short-viewport fallback, never the default layout, so a scroll container is only allowed in a
17:  * file that also gates it on the measured height.
18:  *
19:  * The shared info sheet is exempt by design - it lives in [InfoButton] and may scroll because
20:  * the permission explanations are optional detail, not the step itself.
21:  */

23: 
24:     private val onboardingSources = listOf(
25:         "src/main/java/com/catsmoker/app/screens/permissions/PermissionScreen.kt",

_Thinking:_

**Requesting default-off choices**

The permissions screen already has usage-access controls; I’ll merge foreground observation into that control and add notification-listener access separately from permission to post CatSmoker’s own notifications.

There’s also a consent issue: a default-on switch alone cannot establish affirmative consent for Firebase’s identifier/location-related analytics. [Google Play’s User Data policy](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10144311?hl=en) requires affirmative consent where applicable. I propose keeping the requested defaults as **pending selections**, with clearly labelled affirmative actions before enabling analytics or gaming access. Selection and Android authorization will remain separate.

4: import android.content.Context
5: import android.content.Intent
6: import android.content.res.Configuration
7: import java.util.Locale
8: 
9: /**
10:  * Manual per-app locale wrapping (no appcompat dependency in this app, so
11:  * `AppCompatDelegate.setApplicationLocales` is unavailable).
12:  *
13:  * Both the [android.app.Application] and the activity route `attachBaseContext` through
14:  * [wrap], so Compose resources, ViewModel `getString` calls, services and notifications
15:  * all resolve the saved language. Changing the language restarts the process (the
16:  * Settings/onboarding screens emit that): a bare activity `recreate()` leaves the
17:  * application context — and every cached `getString` in the surviving ViewModels,
18:  * engine state and running services — on the previous language, which is how
19:  * English-selected UI kept showing Arabic until the process died.
20:  */
21: @SuppressLint("AppBundleLocaleChanges")
22: object LocaleHelper {
23: 
24:     /**
25:      * Normalizes a stored tag to one of the shipped locales (`en`, `ar`, `es`, `zh-CN`,
26:      * `pt-BR`) or `""` (system). Region variants (`ar-SA`, `zh-TW`, `pt-PT`) fold to their
27:      * shipped base;
28:      * unknown tags pass through and fall back to English resources at runtime.
29:      * Pure — covered by `LocaleHelperTest`.
30:      */
31:     fun normalizeTag(tag: String): String {
32:         if (tag.isEmpty()) return tag
33:         val base = tag.substringBefore('-').lowercase()
34:         return when (base) {
35:             "ar" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_ARABIC
36:             "es" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_SPANISH
37:             "en" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_ENGLISH
38:             "zh" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_CHINESE
39:             "pt" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_PORTUGUESE
40:             "ja" -> AppearanceStore.LANGUAGE_JAPANESE
41:             else -> tag
42:         }
43:     }
44: 
45:     // In-app switcher covers all API levels with a process restart; no Play Core language delivery.
46:     fun wrap(base: Context): Context {
47:         val tag = normalizeTag(AppearanceStore.languageTag(base))
48:         if (tag.isEmpty()) return base
49:         val locale = Locale.forLanguageTag(tag)
50:         Locale.setDefault(locale)
51:         val config = Configuration(base.resources.configuration).apply {
52:             setLocale(locale)
53:             setLayoutDirection(locale)
54:         }
55:         return base.createConfigurationContext(config)
56:     }
57: 
58:     /**
59:      * Relaunches the app from its launcher intent and ends this process, so the next
60:      * process resolves every resource — activity strings, cached ViewModel strings,
61:      * engine state, services and notification channels — in the newly picked language.
62:      */
63:     fun restartApp(context: Context) {
64:         val launch = runCatching {
65:             context.packageManager.getLaunchIntentForPackage(context.packageName)
66:         }.getOrNull()
67:         if (launch != null) {
68:             launch.addFlags(Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK or Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TASK)
69:             runCatching { context.startActivity(launch) }
70:         }
71:         Runtime.getRuntime().exit(0)
72:     }
73: }

3: import android.service.notification.NotificationListenerService
4: import android.service.notification.StatusBarNotification
5: import com.catsmoker.app.screens.gaming.mode.GamingEngine
6: import dagger.hilt.android.AndroidEntryPoint
7: import javax.inject.Inject
8: import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
9: import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
10: import kotlinx.coroutines.SupervisorJob
11: import kotlinx.coroutines.cancel

13: import kotlinx.coroutines.launch
14: 
15: /**
16:  * Second-layer notification suppression while Gaming Mode is on.
17:  *
18:  * Cross-checked against the reference project's `GamingNotificationListener`
19:  * (`reference/gamingtools/booster`, `com.framex.app.gaming.GamingNotificationListener`): the
20:  * same shape and the same reason to exist. DND is the first layer, but some OEM skins post
21:  * their own "system warning" and battery alerts around the interruption filter — cancelling
22:  * the notification directly does not depend on the filter being honoured.
23:  *
24:  * This service only ever *cancels* what the tray shows: it posts nothing and changes no
25:  * setting, so deactivation needs no restore. When [GamingEngine.notificationSuppressionActive]
26:  * flips off, this listener simply stops cancelling, and anything posted afterwards reappears
27:  * normally. That asymmetry is deliberate — a suppression layer that had to be "undone" would
28:  * need to answer which notifications it ate, which the platform does not offer.
29:  *
30:  * The user must grant notification access (Settings → Special app access → Notification
31:  * access) before the system will bind this service at all. Until then the layer is reported
32:  * by [GamingModeReport.notificationSuppression] as null — "not applicable" — rather than as a
33:  * failure, the same distinction every other report field makes.
34:  */
35: @AndroidEntryPoint
36: class GamingNotificationListener : NotificationListenerService() {
37: 
38:     @Inject lateinit var gamingEngine: GamingEngine
39: 
40:     private val serviceScope = CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.Main)
41: 
42:     override fun onListenerConnected() {
43:         super.onListenerConnected()
44:         // Covers the mid-game grant too: if the user enables notification access while Gaming
45:         // Mode is already on, the tray that filled up before this service existed is purged as
46:         // well, not just notifications posted from now on.
47:         if (gamingEngine.notificationSuppressionActive.value) {
48:             purgeExistingNotifications()
49:         }
50:         serviceScope.launch {
51:             gamingEngine.notificationSuppressionActive.collectLatest { active ->
52:                 if (active) purgeExistingNotifications()
53:             }
54:         }
55:     }
56: 
57:     override fun onDestroy() {
58:         serviceScope.cancel()
59:         super.onDestroy()
60:     }
61: 
62:     override fun onNotificationPosted(sbn: StatusBarNotification?) {
63:         if (!hasConsent()) return
64:         if (sbn == null) return
65:         // Never our own: cancelling the Gaming Mode foreground notification would tear down
66:         // the mode's own stop control, and the overlays' notifications are live telemetry.
67:         if (sbn.packageName == packageName) return
68:         if (gamingEngine.notificationSuppressionActive.value) {
69:             try {
70:                 cancelNotification(sbn.key)
71:             } catch (_: Exception) {
72:                 // Failing to cancel one notification is non-fatal; the next one gets its own try.
73:             }
74:         }
75:     }
76: 
77:     /**
78:      * Walks the current tray and cancels everything that is not ours, the same walk the
79:      * reference performs on activation — DND silences what arrives next, this clears what is
80:      * already on screen.
81:      */
82:     private fun purgeExistingNotifications() {
83:         if (!hasConsent()) return
84:         val current = try {
85:             activeNotifications
86:         } catch (_: Exception) {
87:             return
88:         } ?: return
89:         for (sbn in current) {
90:             if (sbn.packageName == packageName) continue
91:             try {
92:                 cancelNotification(sbn.key)
93:             } catch (_: Exception) {
94:                 // Non-fatal, same as above.
95:             }
96:         }
97:     }
98: 
99:     private fun hasConsent() = com.catsmoker.app.application.privacy.GamingAccessConsent.allows(
100:         this, com.catsmoker.app.application.privacy.GamingAccessConsent.NOTIFICATIONS
101:     )
102: }

### ACTION_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SETTINGS

Source: https://developer.android.com/reference/android/provider/Settings

Activity Action: Show Notification listener settings. In some cases, a matching Activity may not exist, so ensure you safeguard against this. Input: Nothing. Output: Nothing.

```APIDOC
## ACTION_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SETTINGS

### Description
Activity Action: Show Notification listener settings. In some cases, a matching Activity may not exist, so ensure you safeguard against this.

### Constant Value
"android.settings.ACTION_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SETTINGS"

### Input
Nothing.

### Output
Nothing.

### See Also
- NotificationListenerService
```

--------------------------------

### isNotificationListenerAccessGranted(ComponentName listener)

Source: https://developer.android.com/reference/android/app/NotificationManager

Checks whether the user has approved a given NotificationListenerService. This method returns true if the listener has been granted access.

```APIDOC
## isNotificationListenerAccessGranted(ComponentName listener)

### Description
Checks whether the user has approved a given `NotificationListenerService`.

### Method
`boolean isNotificationListenerAccessGranted(ComponentName listener)`

### Parameters
- **listener** (ComponentName) - Required - The component name of the notification listener service.

### Returns
- **boolean** - True if the listener has been granted access, false otherwise.
```

--------------------------------

### isNotificationListenerAccessGranted

Source: https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/android/app/NotificationManager

Checks whether the user has approved a given `android.service.notification.NotificationListenerService`.

The listener service must belong to the calling app.

Apps can request notification listener access by sending the user to the activity that matches the system intent action `android.provider.Settings#ACTION_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SETTINGS`.

--------------------------------

### Check if an app can handle the Intent with resolveActivity() in Kotlin

Source: https://developer.android.com/guide/components/google-maps-intents

Calls resolveActivity() on the Intent to verify a receiving app is installed before calling startActivity(). If the result is null, do not use the intent and disable the feature if possible.

```Kotlin
mapIntent.resolveActivity(packageManager)?.let {
    ...
}
```

--------------------------------

### ACTION_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_DETAIL_SETTINGS

Source: https://developer.android.com/reference/android/provider/Settings

Activity Action: Show notification listener permission settings page for app. Users can grant and deny access to notifications for a ComponentName from here. See android.app.NotificationManager.isNotificationListenerAccessGranted(ComponentName) for more details. Input: The extra EXTRA_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_COMPONENT_NAME containing the name of the component to grant or revoke notification listener access to. Output: Nothing.

```APIDOC

### Description
Activity Action: Show notification listener permission settings page for app. Users can grant and deny access to notifications for a ComponentName from here. See android.app.NotificationManager.isNotificationListenerAccessGranted(ComponentName) for more details.

### Constant Value
"android.settings.NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_DETAIL_SETTINGS"

### Input
The extra EXTRA_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_COMPONENT_NAME containing the name of the component to grant or revoke notification listener access to.

### Output
Nothing.
```

11:  * table at runtime and shows the wrong language. That is how `sys_theme_dynamic` (once absent

D:\Coding\catsmoker\com.catsmoker.app\app\src\main\res\values-zh-rCN\strings_sys.xml:

D:\Coding\catsmoker\com.catsmoker.app\app\src\main\res\values-ja\strings_legal.xml:
  Line 19:     <string name="legal_p_analytics_body">両版に Firebase Analytics が含まれますが、設定で明示的に有効にするまで無効です。以前の既定オンの設定は同意と見なしません。有効時は Google の方針に従い、自動利用イベント、端末とアプリの情報、インストール識別子、IP に基づく概略位置が送られます。アプリ独自のイベントは追加しません。設定で撤回できます。分析への同意は広告の保存やパーソナライズへの同意ではありません。広告の選択は別です。</string>


D:\Coding\catsmoker\com.catsmoker.app\app\src\main\res\values-zh-rCN\strings_legal.xml:
  Line 17:     <string name="legal_p_analytics_body">两种版本都包含 Firebase Analytics，但在您于设置中明确开启前保持关闭。以前默认开启的偏好不视为同意。启用后，Google 会按照其政策接收自动使用事件、设备和应用信息、安装标识符及根据 IP 推算的概略位置。应用不添加自定义事件。可在设置中撤回；分析同意不授权广告存储或个性化，广告选项单独管理。</string>

285:   legacy (see `SPOOF_DEVICE.md`). Inside a target process the same reload also
286:   fills the native tables (`NativeSpoofChannel` → `catsmoker_spoof.c`) that
287:   answer the process's own native property and GL reads.
288: - **Gaming Mode**: snapshot device state → apply levers → report measured
289:   facts → restore from the snapshot (null = don't touch; see `GAMING_MODE.md`).
290: - **Game files**: back up the device's bytes → push → read-back verify
291:   (`md5sum`, never `cat` for bytes; see `FILE_ENGINEERING.md`).
292: - **Scheduled dexopt**: `DexoptSweepScheduler` unique periodic work (`UPDATE`)
293:   → ordinary `DexoptSweepWorker` → `GamingEngine.runArtOptimization` (delegating
294:   to `ArtCompiler`). Cleaner
295:   scheduling is ordinary work too; progress/Stop notifications do not elevate
296:   either worker to a foreground service. System time limits and deferral apply.
297: - **Privacy choices**: `GamingPrivacyGate` records separate, versioned, optional
298:   inventory/foreground-usage/notification-dismissal choices before feature
299:   composition. Readers check `GamingAccessConsent`; retained game-list state is
300:   cleared on withdrawal and watchers stop on their next cycle. Settings can review
301:   or withdraw choices. Android permissions remain a separate prerequisite.
302: - **Analytics**: automatic `FirebaseInitProvider` startup is removed. Both flavors
303:   use `AnalyticsConsent` to initialize Firebase only after a current explicit
304:   Settings opt-in; a legacy default-on flag is not consent. Advertising consent
305:   is independent and Analytics advertising consent types remain denied.
306: - **Play advertising**: the selected `DistributionPrivacyGate` gathers UMP consent
307:   after onboarding. `AdConsentManager` gates SDK initialization and banners on a
308:   successful current update/form result, `canRequestAds`, initialization and
309:   ad-free entitlement. Required privacy options appear in Play settings; Full
310:   has no UMP implementation. Billing acknowledgement is additionally reconciled
311:   by ordinary, network-constrained unique work using fresh Play queries, not
312:   purchase tokens stored in work input.
313: - **Diagnostics**: own-PID logcat → in-memory buffer (`AppLogCollector`) → the
314:   crash handler writes `filesDir/crash_reports` (newest five) → the Logs screen's
315:   Export action builds a ZIP in `cacheDir/shared` and shares it through the
316:   FileProvider (see `LogsViewModel` / `BugReportExporter`).

1: # Design Decisions & Rejected Patterns
2: 
3: Why the codebase is shaped the way it is, and which approaches have already
4: been evaluated and turned down. Entries are deliberately short â€” the operative
5: rules live in the other documents in this folder ([SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md),
6: [SPOOF_DEVICE.md](SPOOF_DEVICE.md), [GAMING_MODE.md](GAMING_MODE.md),
7: [FILE_ENGINEERING.md](FILE_ENGINEERING.md), [CODING_STYLE.md](CODING_STYLE.md));
8: this file records the reasoning and the history so a rejected idea is not
9: re-proposed without new evidence.
10: 
11: When a decision is reversed, add an entry that argues with the old one â€” do
12: not silently rewrite history.
13: 
14: ## Screen-first package ownership (2026-11-12)
15: 
16: - Navigation destinations own peer areas under `features/`: Home, Permissions,
17:   Gaming Tools, Crosshair Library, File Engineering, About, Licenses, Privacy
18:   Policy, Terms, Settings, Logs and Donate. A link from About or Gaming Tools
19:   does not make the linked screen part of the opener's implementation.
20: - Full's six spoof destinations are sibling areas within the spoof feature
21:   family, with explicitly shared state/data/helpers. Existing parent-entry
22:   ViewModel lifetimes and all route values remain unchanged. Embedded game
23:   editors and onboarding steps remain panels/steps, not new destinations.
24: - Retired the generic `shared/data` and `shared/util` buckets. Models belong
25:   to their consuming feature; reused metrics/assets/rendering remain shared;
26:   platform helpers use named `system` responsibilities. The three top-level
27:   ownership roots remain `features`, `shared`, and `system`; no new layers or
28:   modules are needed. See `ARCHITECTURE.md` for the complete map.
